We evaluate LifeMem on Add Health (Harris et al. 2019) and Understandin<sub>g</sub> Societ<sub>y</sub> (Buck and McFall 2012) usi<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>t<sub>une</sub>d LLM<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>es:</sub> Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (hereafter, Llama-8B) (Grattafiori et al. 2024), Ministral-3-8B-Instruct-2512 (Liu et al. 2026), and Qwen3.5-9B (Qwen Team 2026). Com<sub>p</sub>ared to static <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> di<sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y-or</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>promp</sub>ti<sub>ng, non-parame</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>memory, an</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes,</sub> Lif<sub>e</sub>M<sub>em</sub> i<sub>mproves a</sub>li<sub>gn-</sub> <sub>men</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith h<sub>uman</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub> i<sub>n</sub> t<sub>erms o</sub>f <sub>response</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>overa</sub>ll <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group</sub> di<sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-person</sub> <sub>re-</sub> <sub>sponse c</sub>h<sub>ange across</sub> lif<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ages.</sub> O<sub>ur con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons are:</sub>

![](images/8d7b4f4ed8dcbcf0acf31c1da099b6282d21fbf44af83089fa9a341d0a92fb2d.jpg)  
Fi<sub>gure</sub> 1<sub>:</sub> St<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> d<sub>emograp</sub>hi<sub>c con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng pro</sub>d<sub>uces a pa</sub>tt<sub>ern cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y essen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>sm.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>e</sub> 2<sub>,</sub>000 <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub>l<sub>y</sub> sam<sub>p</sub>led WVS wave 7 res<sub>p</sub>ondents (Haer<sub>p</sub>fer et al. 2022) into three socioeconomic status <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>s and com<sub>p</sub>are human res<sub>p</sub>onses with those of profile-conditioned Llama-8B agents. Each individual’s responses are concatenated and projected into a twodimensional PCA space. The separate projections show greater within-group variation and overlap among humans (silhouette −0.02), whereas agents exhibit stronger within-group compression and between-group separation (silhouette 0.19). Silhouette scores are computed in the original response space before PCA projection.

• We identif<sub>y</sub> identit<sub>y</sub> essentialism in static-<sub>p</sub>rofile LLM <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>re</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group</sub> di<sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>exag-</sub> <sub>gera</sub>t<sub>es</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween-group</sub> dif<sub>erences.</sub>

• We <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ose LifeMem<sub>,</sub> combinin<sub>g</sub> structured life-event <sub>memory w</sub>ith <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c parame</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c memory</sub> t<sub>o mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l di<sub>verse an</sub>d <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>s.</sub>

• Across two lon<sub>g</sub>itudinal surve<sub>y</sub>s and three LLMs<sub>,</sub> Life-M<sub>em</sub> i<sub>mproves a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith h<sub>uman</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> di<sub>-</sub> <sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-person</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anges.</sub>

## R<sub>e</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d W<sub>o</sub>rk

C<sub>urren</sub>t LLM<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>soc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>ft<sub>en re</sub>li<sub>es on per-</sub> sona conditionin<sub>g</sub> and static a<sub>g</sub>ent re<sub>p</sub>resentations<sub>.</sub> LLM<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> i<sub>ncreas</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>y use</sub>d <sub>as exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i<sub>pan</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>survey</sub> res<sub>p</sub>ondents (Aher, Arria<sub>g</sub>a, and Kalai 2023; Ar<sub>gy</sub>le et al. 2023<sub>;</sub> S<sub>an</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>k<sub>ar e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2023<sub>;</sub> Bi<sub>s</sub>b<sub>ee e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2024<sub>;</sub> H<sub>u an</sub>d Collier 2024; Lutz et al. 2025). Existin<sub>g</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roaches often <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erv</sub>i<sub>ews,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>es, or</sub> social-media records (Park et al. 2024; Du et al. 2025; L and Conrad 2026; Du et al. 2026; Guo et al. 2026b), su<sub>p</sub>- <sub>por</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> H<sub>owever,</sub> <sub>per-</sub> <sub>sona con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng may re</sub>d<sub>uce response var</sub>i<sub>ance or</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro-</sub> duce bias (Bisbee et al. 2024; Boelaert et al. 2025; Wan<sub>g</sub>, Mor<sub>g</sub>enstern, and Dickerson 2025). Moreover, static <sub>p</sub>rofiles can com<sub>p</sub>ress individuals into demo<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hic t<sub>yp</sub>es (Xie et al. 2026; Hu and Collier 2024). This <sub>p</sub>attern resembles identit<sub>y</sub> <sub>essen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>sm,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>proper</sub>ti<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>soc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l cate<sub>g</sub>ories (Prentice and Miller 2007; Bastian and Haslam

2006), althou<sub>g</sub>h demo<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hic conditionin<sub>g</sub> is not inherentl<sub>y</sub> essentialist (Wan<sub>g</sub>, Mor<sub>g</sub>enstern, and Dickerson 2025). More b<sub>roa</sub>dl<sub>y,</sub> LLM <sub>soc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s are</sub> t<sub>yp</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an up-</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>ll<sub>y or</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> I<sub>n con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>,</sub> l<sub>on-</sub> <sub>g</sub>it<sub>u</sub>di<sub>na</sub>l <sub>researc</sub>h di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h<sub>es</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween-person</sub> dif<sub>erences</sub> f<sub>rom w</sub>ithi<sub>n-person c</sub>h<sub>ange an</sub>d <sub>uses</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d lif<sub>e-even</sub>t hi<sub>s-</sub> tories to better characterize individual trajectories (Curran and Bauer 2011; Elder Jr 1998).

vidual i has a static profile $P _ { i }$ <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>groun</sub>d information and a sequence of life events observed at wave t,

$$

$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $e _ { i , t , k }$ denotes the k-th observed life event and $K _ { i , t } =$ $| \mathcal { E } _ { i , t } |$ . The accumulated trajectory through wave t is

$$
{ \mathcal { T } } _ { i , \leq t } = \bigcup _ { \tau = 1 } ^ { t } { \mathcal { E } } _ { i , \tau } .\tag{2}
$$

## Hi<sub>pp</sub>ocam<sub>p</sub>al Structured Memor<sub>y</sub>

E<sub>ac</sub>h lif<sub>e even</sub>t $e _ { i , t , k }$ i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d f<sub>rom a survey ques</sub>ti<sub>on–</sub> <sub>answer pa</sub>i<sub>r an</sub>d <sub>conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a secon</sub>d<sub>-person s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>emen</sub>t $x _ { i , t , k }$ <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>response.</sub> A f<sub>rozen enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> $g _ { \phi }$ <sub>maps</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>emen</sub>t t<sub>o an em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub>

$$
{ \mathbf { h } } _ { i , t , k } = g _ { \phi } ( x _ { i , t , k } ) .\tag{4}
$$

Th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d <sub>memory s</sub>t<sub>ores eac</sub>h <sub>even</sub>t t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er w</sub>ith it<sub>s</sub> <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>wave</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { M } _ { i , t } ^ { S } = \left\{ \left( e _ { i , \tau , k } , \mathbf { h } _ { i , \tau , k } , \tau \right) | 1 \leq \tau \leq t \right\} .\tag{5}
$$

N<sub>ew</sub> <sub>even</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>appen</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ear</sub>li<sub>er</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>s,</sub> preserving a traceable account of the observed trajectory.

F<sub>o</sub>r <sub>a</sub> <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n $q ,$ <sub>re</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eva</sub>l <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>nes</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>evance</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith t<sub>empora</sub>l d<sub>ecay:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { S c o r e } _ { t } ( q , e _ { i , \tau , k } ) = \sin { ( g _ { \phi } ( q ) , { \bf h } _ { i , \tau , k } ) } \exp [ - \lambda ( t - \tau ) ] ,\tag{6}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\lambda \geq 0$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>recen</sub>t <sub>even</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> d<sub>ecay mo</sub>difi<sub>es re</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eva</sub>l <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub>it<sub>y ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>ng o</sub>ld<sub>er</sub> <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>ences, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e w</sub>h<sub>en su</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y re</sub>l<sub>e-</sub> vant. The top-K events form the retrieved evidence:

$$
R _ { i , t } ( q ) = \mathrm { T o p - K } _ { e \in \mathcal { M } _ { i , t } ^ { S } } \mathrm { S c o r e } _ { t } ( q , e ) .\tag{7}
$$

Th<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>s are</sub> i<sub>nser</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>, prov</sub>idi<sub>ng ques</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>ex-</sub> <sub>pos</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory.</sub>

## C<sub>or</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l P<sub>arame</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> M<sub>emory</sub>

Lif<sub>e</sub>M<sub>em</sub> <sub>ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>er</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>agen</sub>t <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> k<sub>eep</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub> LLM f<sub>rozen.</sub> F<sub>or a</sub> target layer l, the agent-specific weight is

\mathbf { W } _ { i , t } ^ { ( l ) } = \mathbf { W } _ { 0 } ^ { ( l ) } + \frac { \alpha _ { \mathrm { L o R A } } } { r } \mathbf { B } _ { i , t } ^ { ( l ) } \mathbf { A } _ { i , t } ^ { ( l ) } ,\tag{8}
$$

where r and $\alpha _ { \mathrm { L o R A } }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>ran</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>li<sub>ng co-</sub> <sub>e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e use</sub> $\mathbf { \widetilde { \Theta } } _ { i , t } ^ { \mathrm { L o R A } }$ t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e a</sub>ll <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers,</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> $\{ \mathbf { A } _ { i , t } ^ { ( l ) } , \mathbf { B } _ { i , t } ^ { ( l ) } \} _ { l }$ <sub>.</sub> S<sub>epara</sub>t<sub>e a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>ers a</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub> d<sub>emograp</sub>hi<sub>c</sub> <sub>pro</sub>fil<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>op</sub> dif<sub>-</sub> ferent parametric states as their trajectories diverge.

E<sub>ac</sub>h lif<sub>e</sub> <sub>even</sub>t <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances:</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>survey</sub> <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>on–answer</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>preserv</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>response</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>an even</sub>t<sub>-recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> li<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e even</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>-person</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> T<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er,</sub> th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>survey s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>cs.</sub>

Ad<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>sequen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>across</sub> <sub>waves:</sub>

$$
\Theta _ { i , t } ^ { \mathrm { L o R A } , ( 0 ) } = \Theta _ { i , t - 1 } ^ { \mathrm { L o R A } } , \qquad t > 1 ,\tag{9}
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>wave</sub> i<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>er</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> At <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>wave,</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng com</sub>bi<sub>nes</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances</sub> f<sub>rom curren</sub>t <sub>even</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> <sub>sma</sub>ll <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub>t <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay:</sub>

$$

where η controls the contribution of replayed experiences. Th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> f<sub>rozen, an</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e agen</sub>t<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> l<sub>ow-ran</sub>k <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> $\Theta _ { i , t } ^ { \mathrm { L o R A } }$ <sub>are op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d<sub>.</sub> R<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>ay serves as</sub> <sub>a</sub> li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm</sub> f<sub>or m</sub>iti<sub>ga</sub>ti<sub>ng ca</sub>t<sub>as</sub>t<sub>rop</sub>hi<sub>c</sub> f<sub>orge</sub>t<sub>-</sub> <sup>tin</sup>g <sup>rather than a se</sup>p<sup>arate memor</sup>y <sup>com</sup>p<sup>onent</sup>.

## B<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes</sub>

Static conditionin<sub>g</sub> includes Direct and Profile (Ar<sub>gy</sub>le <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2023<sub>;</sub> S<sub>an</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>k<sub>ar e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2023<sub>;</sub> Bi<sub>s</sub>b<sub>ee e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2024<sub>;</sub> H<sub>u</sub> and Collier 2024); diversit<sub>y</sub>-oriented <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> includes Multilingual (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2025) and Anti-Stereotype (Sivakumar et al. 2025); and non-<sub>p</sub>arametric memor<sub>y</sub> includes SimVBG (Du et al. 2025), Full History (Maharana et al. 2024), and Event RAG (Lewis et al. 2020; Maharana et al. 2024). Random Event uses random events to test the importance of respondent-specific trajectories.

## E<sub>va</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> M<sub>e</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs</sub>

We use four lower-is-better metrics. KL divergence measures <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> h<sub>uman an</sub>d <sub>agen</sub>t <sub>response</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> The within-group pairwise distance gap is the absolute diff<sub>erence</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> h<sub>uman an</sub>d <sub>agen</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group pa</sub>i<sub>rw</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>response</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ances.</sub> A <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>er gap</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group</sub> di<sub>vers</sub>it<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s c</sub>l<sub>oser</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f h<sub>umans, sugges</sub>t<sub>-</sub> ing less identity-essentialist behavior. The normalized entropy gap is the absolute diference between the normalized response entropies of humans and agents. The transitiondistribution JS divergence measures how closely the model <sub>repro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>uman popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>re-</sub> sponse transitions between adjacent survey waves.

## I<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> D<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s</sub>

W<sub>e use</sub> thr<sub>ee s</sub>imil<sub>a</sub>rl<sub>y s</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d LLM<sub>s:</sub> Ll<sub>a</sub>m<sub>a-</sub>3<sub>.</sub>1<sub>-</sub>8B<sub>-</sub>In<sub>s</sub>tr<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>,</sub> Ministral-3-8B-Instruct-2512, and Qwen3.5-9B. Usin<sub>g</sub> a <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> <sub>see</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f 42<sub>,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> 100 <sub>respon</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>per</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> All <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e same samp</sub>l<sub>es, ques</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>ones,</sub> and deterministic decoding settings, with temperature 0 and <sub>samp</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> di<sub>sa</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> E<sub>vent</sub> RAG <sub>uses</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ronger</sub> b<sub>ge-m</sub>3 <sub>re-</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ever, w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> Lif<sub>e</sub>M<sub>em uses</sub> th<sub>e</sub> li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>a</sub>ll<sub>-</sub>Mi<sub>n</sub>iLM<sub>-</sub> L6-v2 encoder. Both methods retrieve the top-K = 5 events. LifeMem uses a temporal decay factor of 0.105, rank-8 LoRA adapters, replay size 4, and replay weight 0.5. Experiments <sub>run on one</sub> NVIDIA A800<sub>-</sub>SXM4 80 GB GPU<sub>.</sub>

## Prompt Template

Forget you are an AI model. Simulate a human being.   
Please answer the following question truthfully.

Please answer based on the following personal profile:

You are <gender>. You are aged <age>. You live in <country>. You are <citizenship status>. You were born <birthplace status>. Your mother was born <mother’s birthplace status>. Your father was born <father’s birthplace status>. You live in <urbanicity>, <settlement type>, with a population of <population size>. Your household consists of <household size> members. You <parent cohabitation status>. You speak <home language> at home. You are <marital status> and <children description>. Your education level is <education level>. Your spouse’s education level is <spouse education level>. You are <employment status>. You work as <occupation>. You work in <employment sector>. Your spouse is <spouse employment status>. Your spouse works as <spouse occupation>. You are <chief wage earner status>. During the past year, your family <household financial change>. You belong to <subjective social class>. Your income is in income group <income group> (1 = lowest, 10 = highest). You identify as <religion>. Your ethnic group is <ethnic group>.

Question: <question>

Options: <options>

Please ONLY output a number. Do NOT write any words, symbols, punctuation, or explanations.

The average distance between i and <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er mem</sub>b<sub>ers o</sub>f th<sub>e same group</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
a ( i ) = \frac { 1 } { \left| C _ { i } \right| - 1 } \sum _ { j \in C _ { i } \atop j \not = i } d ( i , j ) .\tag{12}

The minimum average distance between i and any other SES <sub>g</sub>r<sub>oup</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$

The silhouette value for individual i is

$$
s ( i ) = \frac { b ( i ) - a ( i ) } { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ a ( i ) , b ( i ) \} } ,\tag{14}
$$

Structured Question Records. ## Life-Event Variables

Variable: H1GH2

Section: Section 3: General Health

Question: How often have you had a headache?

Options:

0: Never;

1: Just a few times;

2: About once a week;

3: Almost every day;

4: Every day

Variable: H1GH43

Section: Section 3: General Health

Question: During the past 30 days, how often did you drive a car or other vehicle when you had been drinking alcohol?

Options:

0: Never;

1: 1 time;

2: 2 or 3 times;

3: 4 or 5 times;

4: 6 or more times

## Evaluation Variables

Variable: H1ED19

Section: Section 5: Academics and Education

Question: You feel close to people at your school.

Options:

1: Strongly agree;

2: Agree;

3: Neither agree nor disagree;

4: Disagree;

5: Strongly disagree

Variable: H1PF4

Section: Section 18: Personality and Family

Question: You are satisfied with the way your mother and you communicate with each other.

Options:

1: Strongly agree;

2: Agree;

3: Neither agree nor disagree;

4: Disagree;

5: Strongly disagree

## Survey-to-Statement Conversion Prompt

You convert survey question-answer pairs into concise personal statements.

Rewrite the provided question and answer as one faithful second-person statement and one first-person statement about the respondent.

## Rules:

1. Preserve the exact meaning of the answer.

2. Do not add information, explanations, causes, stereotypes, or assumptions.

3. Preserve negation, quantities, dates, frequencies, and time ranges.

4. For life events, do not convert an event into a permanent trait unless the answer says so.

5. Return valid JSON only with keys second\_person and first\_person.

Input: <input>

<sup>F</sup>or exam<sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>e, t<sup>h</sup>e surve<sub>y</sub> res<sub>p</sub>onse

## Example Survey Response

Question: How often have you had trouble falling asleep or staying asleep?

Answer: Never

i<sub>s conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o:</sub>

## Example Converted Statements

Second\_Person: You have never had trouble falling asleep or staying asleep.

First\_Person: I have never had trouble falling asleep or staying asleep.

F<sub>or a response</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $P$ <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e va</sub>lid l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>se</sub>t $\boldsymbol { \ y } _ { t q } ,$ <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

$$
H ( P ) = - { \frac { 1 } { \log | \mathscr { y } _ { t q } | } } \sum _ { y \in \mathscr { y } _ { t q } } P ( y ) \log P ( y ) .\tag{23}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> <sub>zero</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>one</sub> f<sub>or ques</sub>ti<sub>ons w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t t<sub>wo va</sub>lid <sub>response op</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>uman an</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>en</sub>t<sub>rop</sub>i<sub>es separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y:</sub>

$$

Consider demographic variable g, group value v, wave t, and question $q .$ L<sub>e</sub>t $n _ { h } ^ { t q g v } ( y )$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f h<sub>uman</sub> respondents in the group who select option y, and let

$$

$$

F<sub>or groups con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t t<sub>wo va</sub>lid <sub>responses,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>uman</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group ca</sub>t<sub>egor</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rw</sub>i<sub>se</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
D _ { h } ( t , q , g , v ) = 1 - \frac { \sum _ { y \in \mathcal { V } _ { t q } } n _ { h } ^ { t q g v } ( y ) \left( n _ { h } ^ { t q g v } ( y ) - 1 \right) } { n _ { h } ^ { t q g v } \left( n _ { h } ^ { t q g v } - 1 \right) } .\tag{28}
$$

Thi<sub>s quan</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t t<sub>wo</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>t <sub>respon-</sub> d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e same group prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>an-</sub> <sub>swers.</sub> Th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> i<sub>s compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ana</sub>l<sub>ogous</sub>l<sub>y:</sub>

$$
{ \cal D } _ { m } ( t , q , g , v ) = 1 - \frac { \sum _ { y \in \mathscr { y } _ { t q } } n _ { m } ^ { t q g v } ( y ) \left( n _ { m } ^ { t q g v } ( y ) - 1 \right) } { n _ { m } ^ { t q g v } \left( n _ { m } ^ { t q g v } - 1 \right) } .\tag{29}

## M<sub>ec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm</sub> <sub>an</sub>d R<sub>epresen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> A<sub>na</sub>l<sub>yses</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA Vi<sub>sua</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n Pr<sub>oce</sub>d<sub>u</sub>r<sub>e</sub>

T<sub>o v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>li<sub>ze</sub> th<sub>e evo</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c parame</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c mem-</sub> <sub>o</sub>r<sub>y, we ex</sub>tr<sub>ac</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>pa</sub>r<sub>a</sub>m<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub>r<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>eve</sub>r<sub>y age</sub>nt <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>e</sub>r each survey wave. For agent i at wave t, all LoRA parameter t<sub>ensors are</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>tt<sub>ene</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e vec</sub>t<sub>or:</sub>

$$
\pmb \theta _ { i , t } = \mathrm { c o n c a t } _ { m \in \mathcal { M } } \mathrm { v e c } \left( \pmb \Theta _ { i , t } ^ { ( m ) } \right) ,\tag{41}
$$

where M denotes the consistentl<sub>y</sub> ordered set of LoRA tensors, includin<sub>g</sub> the corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> lora\_A and lora\_B

<sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers across a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> U<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>or</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ensures</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on</sub> h<sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> across a<sub>g</sub>ents an<sup>d</sup> waves.

We stack the vectors from all N agents and T waves into <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x:</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbf { X } = \left[ \begin{array} { l } { \pmb { \theta } _ { 1 , 1 } ^ { \top } } \\ { \pmb { \theta } _ { 1 , 2 } ^ { \top } } \\ { \vdots } \\ { \pmb { \theta } _ { N , T } ^ { \top } } \end{array} \right] . } \end{array}\tag{42}
$$

E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>di<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>across</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>–wave</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es:</sub>

$$
X _ { j , d } ^ { \prime } = \frac { X _ { j , d } - \mu _ { d } } { \sigma _ { d } + \epsilon } ,\tag{43}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\mu _ { d }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\sigma _ { d }$ <sub>are</sub> th<sub>e mean an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d d<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f dimension d over all rows of X, and ϵ is a small constant f<sub>or numer</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub> Thi<sub>s s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on preven</sub>t<sub>s</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons w</sub>ith l<sub>arger numer</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>es</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> the projection.

We fit a single two-dimensional PCA projection to the <sub>g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>di<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x:</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbf { Z } = \mathrm { P C A } _ { 2 } ( \mathbf { X } ^ { \prime } ) . } \end{array}\tag{44}
$$

Th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t $\mathbf { z } _ { i , t } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { 2 }$ <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e o</sub>f agent i after wave t. For each agent, states are connected in <sub>c</sub>h<sub>rono</sub>l<sub>og</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>or</sub>d<sub>er,</sub>

$$
{ \bf z } _ { i , 1 }  { \bf z } _ { i , 2 }  \cdot \cdot \cdot  { \bf z } _ { i , T } ,\tag{45}
$$

forming a longitudinal parameter trajectory.
